The second component is the work with the perpetrator. It is important to intervene very quickly in the escalation spiral in order to prevent further violence. That is why in the future, we will force these perpetrators into a corset in so-called force intervention centres and “need them” – under quotation marks – to conduct a corresponding consultation. If this is promising, these people are also working in the field of anti-aggression training.

Lindner: We can do that!]] [[further calls to the SPÖ]] The answer to this question is therefore as unusual as it has been because you are relating two things to each other, which correspond to a comparison of apples with pears: A negative asylum notice is not equivalent to a return decision. But that's what you're mending with each other, and that's why you can't create the statistics you want in this form. There are, as it were, negative asylum decisions in which a return decision is inadmissible. In particular, the provisions of the Convention on Human Rights, such as Article 8. Then there are cases where, as it were, it is temporarily unacceptable to make a return decision. That is when, for example, a family member is affected, so to speak, by illness. There are things in which deportations are inadmissible. This is the famous non-refoulement rule that prevents deportations to Syria, for example. Then there are deportations, which are, as it were, not feasible, simply because there is a lack of acceptance of the opposite. One example of this is the problem of home travel certificates, if States are not prepared to recognise these citizens as their citizens, even in spite of existing readmission agreements. Furthermore, there is the possibility that a person who has a negative asylum notice will avoid deportation. Yes, and then there is the possibility that someone who has a negative asylum notice will leave voluntarily – then he will not be postponed. You see, there are quite a large number of components to be considered in this context. And that's the reason why the request response has looked the way it looks.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-05-16-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00076_d8e3319	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-05-16-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00076_d8e3334	Mr President, I would like to thank you for giving me the opportunity, perhaps with a prejudice, to use the deportation as a preferred variant of the foreign transfer. – The opposite is the case. This has several advantages: On the one hand, it's cheaper. Although we take a certain sum of money into our hands – there is almost a small start-up aid for those who choose a voluntary leave, the higher the earlier someone comes to this realization in the process – this therefore has the advantage of saving ourselves in this case, so to speak, the expensive security costs that a deportation entails. I should like to point out that the new Federal Agency for Assistance and Support Services, which we are going to vote on today, is also explicitly foreseen that there will be no savings in the area of return advice, but, on the contrary, increased investment. There is another advantage: Individual states are more likely to accept people when they return voluntarily; they almost refuse to take back forced displaced people than their citizens. The third advantage is that with financial support for these people a return to their home country in dignity is possible and they can build up a small existence there.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-05-16-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00076_d8e4475	Mr Federal Minister, it was just the talk of the EU election that will take place in ten days. I would have been interested in what measures are being taken to prevent possible influence on the EU election?
ParlaMint-AT_2019-05-16-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00076_d8e4493	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2019-05-16-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00076_d8e4508	When it comes to the issue of influence on EU elections, it is necessary to distinguish between two components. One is the component of information, i.e. what we understand in the broadest sense by disinformation campaigns. The second component is the attempt to have a technical influence on electoral procedures, on data transmissions et cetera. Both components must be taken into account, with only this second, technical component falling within our responsibilities from the point of view of the Interior. We have, of course, taken appropriate measures, first of all in the course of a great exercise in conjunction with the other European countries that took place at the beginning of this year. However, we have also set up an electoral cooperation network in Austria, together with the Federal Chancellor's Office, the Federal Chancellor's Office and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, in which we pay attention to these components, and in particular the national electoral authorities have been visited individually by trained, technically-orientated staff of the BAT and the LVT. This has carried out appropriate risk analyses and consultations where security has to be increased in these areas, in particular where the data integrity is concerned and where the data in connection with the election process is to be provided in a sound manner. In addition, there have also been so-called awareness training courses for individual employees in the electoral authorities. That means we are very, very well positioned on the technical side. What's important to me is: For those who are voters, constituencies or trustees in these elections, the home page of the Ministry of the Interior offers an appropriate e-learning tool, which allows you to work through this task step by step with tasks, a question-and-answer game, and in the end earn a certificate. I believe that this is a very, very good instruction for those who take on this high responsibility.

For the Steirers themselves, it is the following – please, that is with a certain nationalism –: The Steirers say this is for them craftsmanship, because in the DAC regulation – which is why this law is so important – they can pack something else, for example, that the grapes have to be harvested manually, there is no machine harvest. That's something important, that's 200 years of cultural work. The cultural work is for the Styrian wine growers. Even the old Franz Schubert had been drinking Schilcher with the hut burner from Graz 200 years ago. Maybe you should look at Wildbach Castle, which is a very important story. The Steirs have a special terroir, which means that the soil, climate, people, the environment is accessible only in these areas of Eastern Styria, Southern Styria and also the West Styria. The wine-growers are very proud of this, but they also have a flagship of training. Think of Silberberg – internationally far respected! –: These wine growers are specialists who all come from the Weinbauschule Silberberg. Finally – because I am already at the end of the speaking time – you could swallow over the South Styria or the East Styria or the West Styria: Just come, visit and then drink DAC wine! As old Goethe said, What you inherit from your fathers, acquire it to possess it! [[Cheerfulness of the Abg. Straster.]] The Steirs are doing this in the sense of this law and will continue to develop qualitatively. In this sense, we shall also agree. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-05-16-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00076_d8e10129	Madam President. High house! With its quality wine strategy, Austrian wine has certainly become an unprecedented success story. The DAC system opens up another possibility for Austrian wine producers to market wines better. The Wine Act so far allows any quality wine to bear the particulars of large areas, municipalities and Rieden. However, some DAC regulations aim to ensure that quality wines not placed on the market as DAC wines are marketed under the respective wine-growing area name without smaller geographical indications. In order to create legal certainty for this DAC Regulation, a novelle to the Wine Law is required. If the DAC regulations do not provide for such a possibility, large areas, wine-growing communities and Rieden can continue to be specified. I am therefore particularly pleased that this novice of the Wine Law can take another important step for the local wine growers. Quality and origin are even more clearly linked in the future – if the region so decides unanimously in the Wine Committee. Thank you very much.

There are experiences throughout Europe: In 2018, 10,000 sheep were torn in France, over 200 sheep were torn in South Tyrol, and in Germany there were hundreds of cracks of farm animals. In Switzerland, which has more than 20 years of experience in herd protection, it has been decided last week that it needs withdrawals because otherwise one can no longer be mastered by the wolf. That is why, I believe, it is quite appropriate for us to deal with it. Wolf populations are growing relatively rapidly. A wolf needs between 1,000 and 1,500 kilos of meat in the year, so you can see that he needs a food supply and it's about making a balance in this regard. From my point of view, it is very important that we accompany the measures taken by the Federal Ministers in this regard, that we do not allow the resettlement of the wolf to be left open and unbridled, but that we develop measures and protect our domestic and farm animals. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the FPÖ.]] The wolf has a very strict protection status, but it has no natural enemies. One luggages and impoverishes things and emphasizes that there are compensation payments. ParlaMint-AT_2019-05-16-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00076_d8e13678	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! High house! Appreciate Seers! Yes, the wolf is back in Austria, and it seems he wants to stay. Wolf’s theme arouses strong emotions, all the more so More important is an objective approach to this subject, without euphoria and without inhibiting fear. Today, the wolf population in Europe – depending on whether or not to include the European part of Russia – is about 10,000 to 19 000 individuals. The wolf is an internationally strictly protected animal species, which is essential for Europe, the Fauna-Flora-Habitat Directive, which has been transposed into the laws of nature conservation and the hunting laws of the countries in Austria. The aim of the Fauna-Flora-Habitat Directive is to preserve or achieve a favourable conservation status for certain wild species of animals and plants. These measures are important and have their rights undisputed. A clear red line will be crossed for me, however, when the Austrians are left alone with their real concerns and needs. Explained environmental romance is not in place. The return of the wolf means considerable financial losses for our farmers and for the hunting sector, which can sometimes reach even threat of existence. The concern for the safety of people who want to use nature for recreational purposes is also justified. In my home community of Allensteig, a wolf pack was first found in 2016, which has provided young people with a litter since each year. The Allentsteig Troop Exercise Square offers the wolf a sufficiently large area due to its expansion of approximately 157 square kilometres – for a wolf pack. In this case, a life with the wolf works almost unchallenged. Together with the State of Lower Austria and the University of Vienna, a research project was launched to gain important insights into the wolf and its influence on the Red Wild. The wolves there have been equipped with transmitters to gain information about their hiking movements during the search for the territory. Finally, it is only the legislator who has to create the framework conditions for a reasonable co-existence between man and Wolf. Although the countries and the Federal Government are endeavouring to provide rapid assistance with advice, compensation and the promotion of protective measures, one must be clear: An Austrian single entrance to the relief of the wolf's protection status is not possible due to the requirements from Brussels. Ladies and gentlemen! The wolf is European and has benefited from European travel freedom since the fall of the Iron Curtain at the latest. [[Cheerfulness at the FPÖ.]] For this reason, I call on the competent decision-makers and ask the European Union to have more regional scope for the defence of problem wolves. Thank you.

In my opinion, we need balanced access, the restriction on only one region or only a specific group is not targeting. On the question 9: There was already a joint strategy conference on misinformation during the Austrian Presidency. This has been explicitly praised by the participants. In autumn 2019, there is also a training offer for combating misinformation in the Western Balkans, because it is important for us to make a contribution beyond the European borders. Question 10: The Austrian Federal Government is well aware of the threat posed by disinformation. We are therefore driving forward the implementation of the Action Plan, and it is generally about greater transparency and responsibility in the Internet ecosystem, especially in relation to online platforms. On the question 11: The Federal Ministry of Europe, Integration and External Affairs is in contact with the Task Force for Strategic Communications as well as with the embassies and EU delegations of the respective states. Cooperation works very well from our point of view and is constantly being improved. Question 12: The Austrian position continues to apply in the budget negotiations – you will not be surprised. On the question 13: We are firmly implementing the action plan at national level. The competent colleagues in the Federal Chancellery, in the departments and our national contact point are in constant exchange and contribute to the work of the Electoral Cooperation Network. Our aim is to raise awareness among all stakeholders of targeted disinformation campaigns from third countries and, above all, to support and strengthen the free press in their important task. In this context, I am glad that a recent RTR-oriented event format on disinformation has taken place in the media. Question 14: Media competence and critical thinking are prerequisites in the effort against disinformation, whether domestic or foreign. Measures to strengthen media competence must therefore be integrated into all sectors. Only yesterday, for example, the increase of the private broadcasting fund from currently EUR 15 million to EUR 20 million was decided annually, with a focus on promoting media competence in the field of moving images, also to combat manipulation and disinformation. Questions 15, 16 and 17: At the time, the 2012 Party Act was declared a milestone in the development of the political system in Austria by all parties. ÖVP, SPÖ and Greens have negotiated and decided together. All political groups have always agreed that this is a task for Parliament and not for the government. I therefore think it desirable that, in future, the parliamentary clubs agree with each other and try not only to discuss these issues, but also to find common solutions in the case of possible further developments. On the question 18: Freedom of the press and of expression is one of the highest values of our democracy and of our Western world. In general, policy has nothing to do with the employment of journalists in the media or with reporting. In order to ensure free journalism in the best possible way, we have even excluded journalistic activity from the GDPR, which I consider to be correct. [[Intrusion of the Abg. These media can – I give you the right – also be misused to spread misinformation specifically in order to influence public opinion in one direction or another, to adapt and defamate specific groups. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Especially with regard to the EU election, I share the view that there are forces within and outside Europe that use classical and modern media to disseminate, scatter and influence the outcome of elections. But it is also the targeted attacks on our electoral systems that we have to deal with and deal with. The action plan against disinformation, presented within the framework of the Austrian Presidency, has already been addressed. This provides for an early warning system on disinformation, in particular for the exchange between Member States and also for representatives in the Member States. It is also very important, I believe, when it comes to social media, to the code of conduct established last year and to ensure more transparency and objective information and to oppose disinformation. In the course of this EU election, one also sees that this code of conduct is taking effect. Several groups have been blocked and false profiles, fake accounts, have been deleted. There is also, above all, a transparent information about who receives an advertisement and why. I believe that all these are important steps that must continue to be pursued. Of course, we still learn to gain experience on a daily basis and develop these measures further. We need to work together in this area at EU level, but we also do a lot at europ - i.e. at Austrian level. [[Abg. Vogl: Is the same thing anyway!]] However, I would also like to say that it cannot be the policy alone that decides what is true or what is wrong. On the contrary! We need independent fact-finding independent institutions, independent media and independent scientists. What I do not want is a European or state authority – a censorship authority – that decides what is right or what is wrong on the Internet. What do we do instead? – We bring journalists to a table with researchers, with auditors, for example through our regulatory authority RTR. We have used the media competence week in March 2019 to also give the pupils and teachers a hand in the field of disinformation teaching materials and to design them in concrete terms one week on the topic of disinformation in order to prepare students for the choice of Europe. However, we do this not only for students, but also for older target groups via Fit4Internet, for example; courses are offered to teach basic knowledge, but they also go so far as to force a critical discussion with online content. Those who have listened properly today have also learned that Federal Minister Kickl has said that all ministries are working together at the technical level to identify threat scenarios, to set up measures, to train employees properly, and to exchange information within this cooperation network. I should also like to mention the protection of critical infrastructure, concerning which last year we adopted the NIS law, and it is also an important factor in ensuring security. There are some emergency teams in Austria, which are available mainly to the industries, but also to the public in order to react quickly in the case of cases. Yesterday – many have also expressed positive views on this matter – the funding for the private media has been increased from EUR 15 million to EUR 20 million. It is intended to promote TV formats that promote media competence. This is a positive approach to ensuring the diversity of information. At the end of this debate, I can see that I do not think it has contributed much to safety or clarification for the electorate. Nevertheless, I say that thanks be to God the Federal Government has put forward many measures in advance and is working on this too. ParlaMint-AT_2019-05-16-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00076_d8e21556	Madam President. Madam President of the Court of Auditors! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) High house! After the topic of Wolf, we are now returning to the normal agenda. Today we are dealing with a report by the Court of Auditors on transport infrastructure in Austria. It is about planning, expanding and financing. The Court of Auditors carried out the strategic audit for the years 2002 to 2015, during which the period under review covered the years 2011 to 2015. Overall, this review has included several priorities: the requirements for planning and financing in the high-level transport infrastructure sector, the process of network planning, the strategic audits in the field of network changes, the prioritisation of network changes and the financing and maintenance of the expansion of high-level transport infrastructure. The Court of Auditors has adopted a total of 23 final recommendations. From 2011 to 2015, a total of EUR 15.2 billion was invested in the high-level transport network in Austria, of which EUR 3.1 billion in 2015 alone. The President of the Committee has noted that in future, especially as regards enlargement, the legal requirements must be adapted and improved. As a positive point, the Court of Auditors has mentioned that there are currently updates as regards traffic forecasts, because, of course, things are changing very quickly. In future, this will be adjusted every three to five years. An important goal of the Ministry – and this is important for me as a Tyrolean and as someone who naturally meets the subject of traffic every day in life, of course – is the shift of freight traffic from road to rail. To this end, Federal Minister Hofer mentioned in the Committee that there will certainly be priorities in the future, particularly as regards the expansion of rail capacity in Austria. It is clearly an objective – and this has been formulated here in the Committee by Federal Minister Hofer – that freight transport in Austria should be increased to the 40 percent mark by 2025. The expansion of the Koralm tunnel connections and of course Brenner will have priority. Finally, it can be said that in this report, the Court of Auditors has identified ways of improving the development of the high-level transport network. He also pointed out quite clearly that in the future we will have to vote in a more strategic and targeted manner, and that we will also have to be very clear and prioritized in moving goods from road to rail. We must take account of this and give priority to it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2019-05-16-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00076_d8e21732	Madam President. Madam President-in-Office of the Council! Ladies and gentlemen! Plessl: Why hasn't it been written out yet?]] The Federal Ministry of Transport, Innovation and Technology, the Federal Ministry of Sustainability and Tourism as well as the Asfinag, the State of Lower Austria, the City of Vienna and the Federal Environment Agency were reviewed. [[New call for tender of the Abg. Plessl.]] The period under review essentially covers the years 2009 to 2016. The The objectives of this audit were, in particular, the legal basis for post-control and the verification of compliance with the EIA requirements. The authority to carry out the post-control of the EIA procedure is the Federal Ministry of Transport, Innovation and Technology, together with the participating authorities, namely water law, nature conservation and forestry authorities. That is why our transport minister Norbert Hofer was present in the Committee's deliberations. This audit by the Court of Auditors has, in particular, affected old EIA procedures. The procedures are complicated and technically very complex. They should therefore be made easier, according to the Minister of Transport. Much has already changed in the EIA procedure compared to the investigation period. Necessary requirements for evidence assurance and monitoring have already been prescribed in the current EIA reports, and there is a database for post-control. As Minister Norbert Hofer pointed out in the Committee, after-checking is not a clear, explicit European requirement. It therefore welcomes an evaluation of the system of re-checking, as the Ministry considers this to be gold plating. The recommendations of the Court of Auditors are already being implemented by BMVIT. In the case of current EIA projects, the Asfinag companies shall prepare the status reports quarterly, also based on the database solutions proposed by the Court of Auditors, first and then send them to the Ministry for Examination. There are conditions, deadlines and, subsequently, mandatory controls for this purpose. It has changed a lot since the Court of Auditors examined the EIA procedure. Everything has become clearer, more transparent and can be seen on the website of the transport mini- BMVIT has made a complicated system a little more fit – thanks to the Minister for Transport.

Since 2005, ÖBB has had the goal of building a ticket distribution system that is at the level of time that is simple and understandable for customers. According to the Court of Auditors' report, for this purpose, costs of EUR 131 million were incurred during the project period – the project period from 2005 to 2017. This is, of course, a high amount for all of us, but it must not be overlooked that technical progress has been significant in this period, especially as regards mobile phones. I am already looking to the future and an important recommendation from the Court of Auditors: The more complex the tariff structures and the more different the regulations of the individual allies are, the more expensive the picture is in distribution systems. In Austria we have the collective structure of the ÖBB. We have seven transport associations and, of course, the mobility service providers in the cities. The aim must be – and the Court of Auditors has also formulated it accordingly – to achieve a uniform tariff model as possible. This is why the current plans for a uniform tariff model for Austria are absolutely welcome. Countries, transport associations, the ÖBB and the urban transport companies are already working intensively under the project title ÖV 2020, i.e. public transport 2020, as provided for in the government programme, on a simple, transparent and Austrian-wide tariff model. The Court of Auditors has also found that the ÖBB ticket shop is the most extensive ticket distribution system that has been offered in Austria so far for the purchase of tickets for public transport. This technical platform could then be the basis for an Austrian-wide ÖV ticket system, with the Court of Auditors recommending a neutral ownership structure. One thing is clear: It needs to be easier to buy a ticket. Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! We need to make the distribution systems of public transport tickets even more transparent and even easier in the sense of railway drivers and taxpayers. Thank you very much.

In addition, there is a wealth of reduction variants and additional functions, which has a negative effect on usability. Since many problems arise directly or indirectly from the complexity of the ticket shop, as well as the Court of Auditors, a simpler pricing structure would increase the transparency of ticket prices in the interests of passengers. Ladies and gentlemen! ParlaMint-AT_2019-05-16-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00076_d8e23516	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! I should like to address a citizens' initiative that encourages the preparation of a “map to the transition to animal testing, testing and training” – for example, an initiative by the Dutch government that has set itself the goal of being animal-free by 2025. Mr Bernhard, I would like to do exactly what you have suggested here that we discuss these issues objectively, consider them for and against. Opinions from the Ministry of Science, the University Conference and the Austrian Science Council have been obtained. In principle, the goal, I think, is to support. We will have consensus that the goal must be and will also be that we are on the go without animals. I would like to highlight briefly the situation in Austria: Basic access is a balance of interests regarding the damage that animals need to be added in order to be able to generate a certain benefit for humans or, most of the time, for other animals; I am thinking of veterinary medicine. The above-mentioned catalogue of criteria for the objectivisation and transparency of this damage-benefit analysis is required by law. The criticism that this is sometimes not done exactly or as well, I think, because this form, which needs to be completed, requires a verbal justification. If we consistently implement the three principles – the essential level of benefit to the benefit, the lowest possible load on the animals and the smallest possible number of experimental animals in relation to the gain of knowledge – we will minimise the number of animal experiments in a way that is best possible in the present situation, I think. Research projects are also supported by the Ministry of Science, with the aim that animal experiments are no longer necessary. If a replacement for animal testing is possible as a result of scientific research, animal testing may not be carried out either. On statistics, on the increasing number of animal experiments that have been reported: This is more due to the tendency to go more into basic research. Research is not linear. There is an increase in animal testing in basic research, and the other is a statistical element that owes to the Animal Test Law that transgenic animals kept in breeding are included in the statistics in particular – therefore, we have a statistically increasing number of animal experiments. In fact, this is to be relativized. The Dutch paper also says that it is not possible in basic research until 2025, but it provides a longer horizon. In principle, it should also be noted that due to the scientific progress of biotechnology in combination with digitalization and computer science, we are on the way to a non-animal science. This is a matter of time; in principle, this objective is absolutely in favour. This is a positive aspect, because of scientific progress we will become free from animal testing. Our legal regulations are sufficient in themselves if they are consistently and correctly implemented, and in this sense this citizens' initiative has also been taken note of. Thank you.
